# Test preservation ledger — simplify-model-runtime (task 2.1)

This ledger was created after apply approval, during the implementation phase. It inventories the predecessor tests **before any deletion**. For each collected test node it records one of three dispositions: keep, rewrite or retire. It also lists the new tests planned so far. The ledger adds no decision beyond the frozen proposal, design (Decision 6), the nine specs, `tasks.md` and ADR-0040–0044. No test, code or configuration file was changed to produce it.

## Baseline and evidence

- Base commit: `2c00e608beecc315d60f18ae01eff8932f77854d`.
- Worktree: the isolated worktree `apply-simplify-model-runtime`.
- Virtual environment: `/Users/paulpronko/Documents/develop/smallpieclaw/.venv` (Python 3.14).
- Baseline `make check`: **5397 passed, 2 skipped, 2 warnings**. Ruff and Vulture both pass.
- Evidence directory: `/private/var/folders/6s/p9xmtzd53wv7jx8zq89wqzmr0000gn/T/opencode/simplify-apply-baseline/`. It holds `make-check.log`, `ruff.log`, `vulture.log` and `collect.txt`.
- Collection: `pytest --collect-only -q tests` (collect only, no run) found **5399 nodes** in 165 test modules.
- Baseline skips: the two skips are intentional or depend on the platform. Neither relates to this change.
  - `tests/test_path_policy.py::TestIsContained::test_normcase_case_insensitive` is skipped on macOS (normcase behaviour).
  - `tests/test_consent_scope_wording.py::test_vision_query_scope_line` is an intentional placeholder skip.
  - Both stay classified as keep.
- Rules:
  - There is no target test count, and **no new skips or xfails** are allowed.
  - A retirement removes only an assertion of obsolete policy.
  - Before its source file is deleted, every retained assertion must have an owner suite (tasks 2.2–2.4 and 10.1–10.3).

## Dispositions

| Disposition | Meaning | Nodes |
|---|---|---|
| keep | The node stays unchanged. Its destination is either the current node (kept in place) or a named owner suite (relocated in tasks 2.2, 2.3 or 2.4; `PHASE2` rows then rewrite the old preview-fit entry point). | 4501 |
| rewrite | The node, or a named new owner node, keeps the listed assertions. The new contract replaces the obsolete policy part. | 496 |
| retire | The row gives the exact obsolete-policy reason and the requirement or ADR. Where a replacement applies, it is an add row or a rewritten node. | 402 |
| add | A concrete new node that is planned. Add rows are listed at the end and are not implemented yet. | 47 |

How the nodes were classified:

- 2762 nodes: matched an explicit node rule or file rule.
- 1916 nodes: kept in their owner suites. These nodes keep their behavioral assertions, but many rely on shared or module-local helpers that require rewrites in their owning slices.
- 721 nodes in 40 modules: the file-level symbol scan found no affected module/key reference; the body-level scan below also covers them.

The complete mapping for every node is in `test-preservation-ledger.json`. Its schema is:

- `groups[file][class-id]` lists node suffixes.
- `classes[]` gives the disposition, destination, assertion and references for each class id.

## Audit (recomputable)

```bash
PATH=/Users/paulpronko/Documents/develop/smallpieclaw/.venv/bin:$PATH
T=/private/var/folders/6s/p9xmtzd53wv7jx8zq89wqzmr0000gn/T/opencode/simplify-apply-baseline/ledger_tool
python -I $T/build_ledger.py build <collect.txt> openspec/changes/simplify-model-runtime/test-preservation-ledger.json <table.md>
python -I $T/build_ledger.py audit openspec/changes/simplify-model-runtime/test-preservation-ledger.json
```

The audit recollects the tests with pytest. It then checks that:

- the ledger's node set equals the live collected set;
- no node appears twice;
- every row has a disposition, a destination and an assertion;
- every retire row has destination `-`;
- every add row is still marked as planned.

The build step also uses the Python AST to resolve the function and class path of every node.

Result when the ledger was created: `live=5399 ledger=5399 problems=[]`, with 0 AST-unresolved nodes. The classification rules are in `$T/rules.py`. The tool lives outside the repository because of the write-scope constraint. The parent can move it into version control later.

## Body-level review and helper dependencies (B1–B5 correction)

The initial name/file rule classifier was not sufficient: the review identified guards hidden in generically named tests. The second pass now scans the AST of **every one of the 5,399 collected test bodies**, their decorators and the module-local/shared helpers they call, for the removed-symbol needles shown in JSON `body_scan.needles`. It records the exact `(needle, where)` pairs for each hit in `body_scan.hits`; `build_ledger.py audit` recomputes them. The audit fails if any keep-in-place test has a direct-body removed-symbol hit without a `BODY-OK` explanation, or a helper hit without a named rewrite contract. This is a static source review, not an assertion that tests have already moved or passed under the new policy.

- 2,584 nodes have one or more direct-body/helper hits. All keep-in-place direct-body hits of retired-symbol needles are now either rewritten or explicitly `BODY-OK`: the 13 retained hits assert rejection/absence of obsolete keys, or the **implicit** Main gate. The one `danger` hit is a benign word in an unrelated shell/security test. None is classified keep-in-place merely because its filename is generic.
- The shared/module-local helper rewrite contracts are JSON `body_scan.helper_contracts` and `body_scan.harness_tags`. **F1**: `tests/conftest.py` `minimal_config`/`make_builtin_executor`, `tests/run_model_fixtures.py` `make_run_model_client`, and module-local `AGENT_CFG`/`AGENT`/world builders replace removed `ctx_max_tokens`, `default_model`, `background_model`, `fallback_models` with supported keys. **F2**: `tests/execution_harness.py` `ScriptedLLM`, `NativeScriptedLLM`, `scripted_preview`, `attach_preview`, and module-local preview stubs lose `preview_request`/request-fit hooks without altering retry/checkpoint assertions. **F3**: `tests/root_startup_harness.py` real root composition moves off `ModelSettings`/`models-config` and uses preference/cache plus explicit auxiliary config. **F4-catalog/XDG/snapshot**: module-local catalog/world/XDG/snapshot helpers update to the new contracts. **F4-benign**: module constants list obsolete keys only for rejection/raw-TOML preservation.
- A keep node with a helper tag has destination `same`, but **does not mean its fixture can remain byte-for-byte unchanged**: change the helper in the mapped slice, rerun the consumers and preserve their assertions. A rewrite node is individually reclassified for a changed assertion. `test_scheduler_toml_encoder.py` preserves raw unknown TOML values type-strictly, not legacy execution.
- In `test_request_preparation.py`, 17 nodes are kept. 16 real-client/checkpoint cases relocate in 2.2. The compaction-cancellation node relocates in 2.3 to the context owner suite (refs 2.3, 6.6). The 16 then `PHASE2` rewrites `_prepare_request(ctx,state,system,effective_ctx,...)` calls when preview-fit is retired in 6.7/10.1; wire identity, native grouping, malformed zero-HTTP, timeout checkpoint and strict-resume assertions survive. They are **not** promised to remain verbatim forever.

- **Moved intact; rewrites deferred.** The 13 rewrite nodes still assert today's legacy behaviour (they still read the JSON file). They moved verbatim so that deleting the legacy module in 8.4/10.3 cannot drop their generic legs: atomic TOML change/removal, legacy byte preservation, startup notice and origin-by-TOML. The rewrite itself (existence-only detection, no shadow preflight, obsolete fields as `config_error`) stays in 8.3/8.4 and was **not** implemented here. Each owner section has a comment naming the deferred task and ledger classes.
- **Unchanged assertions.** An AST comparison covered the 10 moved test functions/methods and 6 moved data tables (`_REMOVAL_ALLOWED`, `_SHADOW_CHANGES`, `_BAD_LEGACY`, `_SPOOF_TOML`, `_SHOW_FIELDS`, `_LIST_FIELDS`). Bodies, decorators, parametrize ids and arguments are identical after the mechanical rename `_X` → `legacy_fx.X` and `make_scheduler` → `make_legacy_scheduler`. The rename avoids collisions with the owners' own `_TOML`, `_SECRET`, `_tags` and `make_scheduler`. The 13 test functions left in the source are AST-identical to baseline.
- **Shared legacy fixture.** The legacy data constants, helpers, `_Env` snapshot and Scheduler factory moved to a new non-test module, `tests/scheduler_legacy_fixtures.py`. They are AST-identical to baseline apart from the public names. The only fixture change inlines the former `isolated_schedule` fixture: the same three `monkeypatch` steps run in the same order, before the XDG setup. The source module and each owner wrap `legacy_scheduler_factory` in a function-scoped fixture; the source keeps its `make_scheduler` name, so its 27 retire nodes are unchanged. The module is listed in `support_fixtures`. 3.2 keeps its `models_config_file` assertion valid, 8.4 rewrites the consumers, and 10.3 deletes it.
- **Body-hit scan.** Hits were recomputed for every node in the six edited test files. The only change: the 43 legacy nodes' `models_config` / `helper:make_scheduler` hit is no longer visible, because the static scan does not follow the cross-module factory. To keep the contact on record, each of the 43 live ids has a `body_scan.manual_tags` entry (F4-xdg, recheck in 3.2). The audit tool and its exemptions are unchanged. Result: `live=5403 ledger=5403 counts={keep:4505,rewrite:496,retire:402} adds=47 problems=[]`.
- **Evidence.** Copied-tree mutants all failed on assertions and passed again once restored: legacy bytes clobbered after the atomic TOML write, `os.replace` skipped, reload latch never cleared, recorded-launch-failure `last_run` dropped, legacy notice suppressed, extra public show field. Evidence: `/private/var/folders/6s/p9xmtzd53wv7jx8zq89wqzmr0000gn/T/opencode/simplify-task-2.4/`.

## Task 3.1 execution record

Task 3.1 made `[providers.<connection-id>]` a startup-fixed, read-only map and removed live connection replacement (`ModelRegistry.replace_connections`) and the connection fingerprint and configuration-generation rechecks. The JSON key `executed_3_1` holds the full record. Counts are now 4509 keep, 496 rewrite and 392 retire. Collection is 5397, which is the 5403 baseline minus 10 deleted retire nodes plus 4 new keep nodes. The 47 planned add rows are unchanged.

- **Deleted retire nodes (10).** Each node's ledger class was already `retire` with destination `-`. Each one asserts only live replacement or the fingerprint/generation recheck.
  - `tests/test_model_registry.py`: `test_changed_or_deleted_connection_discards_inflight_refresh_and_purges_inventory` ×2, `test_connection_change_while_resolving_prevents_retargeted_admission` and `test_explicit_admission_rechecks_active_main_after_other_model_credential_resolution` (refs 3.1,4.1). Also `test_scheduled_admission_rejects_state_changed_during_credential_resolution[explicit-provider-endpoint-replaced]`. That node's 4 other params stay, running, until 4.1.
  - `tests/test_graph_model_resolution.py` (refs 5.2): `test_graph_target_aba_restoration_still_fails_generation_recheck`, `test_graph_target_change_during_credential_resolution_fails_connection_changed[target-endpoint-replaced, target-provider-removed]` and `test_unrelated_change_during_credential_resolution_keeps_graph_pin_with_current_provenance[main-provider-removed, main-provider-reconfigured]`. These are retired early because their only mechanism, connection replacement, no longer exists. The record-change params and the other unrelated-change params stay, running, until 5.2.

## Task 3.3 execution record

Task 3.3 added the durable selection preference: `model_settings.ModelPreference` reads and writes only `model = "<connection>/<upstream>"` at `paths.model_selection_file`, by temp file, `fsync` and `os.replace`. `ModelRegistry(preference=...)` writes it before publishing a deliberate `select`; `main.py` passes it for the root registry. The JSON key `executed_3_3` holds the full record. Counts are now 4522 keep, 496 rewrite and 392 retire. Collection is 5410 (5398 + 12 new keep nodes). No node was deleted or retired.

- **Added keep nodes (9, class 518, basis `added-3.3`) in `tests/test_model_settings.py`.** `test_preference_write_before_publish_keeps_prior_bytes_on_failure` (×4: existing/absent × fsync/replace failure) and `test_deliberate_select_replaces_corrupt_preference_atomically` (×4 corrupt kinds) implement the two planned 3.3 add rows. `test_preference_wire_is_exactly_one_selector_without_credentials` implements the preference half of the planned `test_preference_and_cache_contain_no_key_reference_or_endpoint` row; the cache half stays with 3.4. The `adds[]` rows keep `planned-not-implemented` because the unchanged audit tool requires it.
- **Added keep nodes (3, class 519, basis `added-3.3`) in `tests/test_model_registry.py`.** They drive a real registry over real files: the preference is replaced before the active choice is published (observed at the `os.replace` boundary); a failed replace keeps the prior active choice, remembered id and bytes; the catalog `default_model` is neither source nor sink; a corrupt preference survives construction and refresh unchanged and a deliberate `select` replaces it at once. The helper `_make_registry` carries the F4-catalog tag.
- **Rewrite in place (5 nodes, dispositions unchanged).** `tests/test_main.py::TestRootStartupComposition::test_startup_refreshes_every_provider_through_all_pages_then_restores_main`, `::test_missing_main_keeps_commands_available_without_default_or_model_work[absent|removed]`, `::test_models_selection_callback_recovers_ordinary_request_on_same_root_registry` and `tests/test_run_model_admission.py::test_root_pins_survive_later_main_selection_and_real_refresh` now assert the selection in the preference file and `default_model is None` in the catalog. All other assertions are unchanged.
- **F3 helper adaptation.** `tests/root_startup_harness.py` `RootHarness.start` seeds the catalog with `default_model=None` and writes `saved_default` with the real `ModelPreference`. The real `main.py` composition is kept.
- **Intermediate boundary.** Registries built without `preference` (unit suites not yet rewritten, read-only backfill) keep the predecessor `default_model` behaviour until their owner tasks rewrite them (3.4–3.5, 5.3). Catalog inventory is still written to `models-config.toml` until 3.4.

The converter's operation is unchanged until 10.2. `tests/test_migrate_models.py::test_apply_seeds_absent_settings_with_empty_catalog` (retire, unchanged disposition) gained output assertions and a no-preference-file check; its body hits gained `default_model`. `test_deliberate_selection_is_durable_before_publication_and_failure_keeps_prior_choice` additionally asserts `selection_revision` is unchanged after a failed select. README gained a matching note under the migration section.

## Task 3.4 execution record

Task 3.4 added the disposable discovery cache: `model_settings.ModelCache` reads and writes `paths.models_cache_file` (JSON: `schema_version`, per-provider state, provider-reported display data only; no selection, revision, supplement, provenance, models.dev field, key, key reference or endpoint). `load` treats missing/unreadable/invalid as absent without touching bytes; `write` is atomic (temp file, `fsync`, `os.replace`). `ModelRegistry` exposes a bounded `cache_notice` for an unusable cache, and `refresh` now publishes successful discovery in memory even when the cache write fails (reported via `persisted=False`, never touching preference, active choice or revisions). `main.py` wires `ModelCache(paths.models_cache_file)` instead of the combined `models-config.toml`, which the root no longer opens.

- **Added keep nodes (43, classes 520-522, basis `added-3.4`)** in `tests/test_model_settings.py` (34), `tests/test_model_registry.py` (8) and `tests/test_main.py` (1). They implement planned adds `test_cache_write_failure_leaves_selection_unchanged`, `test_corrupt_cache_is_absent_with_actionable_notice` and the cache half of `test_preference_and_cache_contain_no_key_reference_or_endpoint`; the `adds[]` rows stay `planned-not-implemented` because the unchanged audit requires it.
- **Renamed rewrite nodes (class 129, dispositions unchanged).** `test_refresh_write_failure_does_not_publish_additions_or_metadata[addition|metadata]` -> `..._publishes_discovery_in_memory_and_keeps_disk_and_selection[...]`; `test_refresh_write_failure_removal_still_blocks_future_admission` -> `..._removal_is_published_but_keeps_active_choice`; `test_scheduled_admission_rejects_same_revision_removal_only_document_during_resolution` -> `..._rejects_document_published_despite_cache_write_failure_during_resolution`. Rationale: the cache is advisory, so a failed cache write no longer withholds usable discovery. Kept: no disk change, selection/preference unchanged, safe error detail, scheduled-admission `selection_changed`. After oracle correction a failed cache write also keeps the active selector (`CatalogView.active_model_id` now reports the honest, possibly uncached selector; admission still fails closed), and `ModelRegistry.refresh` rejects any provider text containing the resolved credential (`invalid_inventory`).
- **Rewrite in place.** `tests/test_telegram_models.py::TestRefreshPersistenceHonesty::test_unpersisted_refresh_is_not_silently_treated_as_success[command|callback]` (class 495): still asserts an explicit "not saved" notice and untouched bytes; now asserts the new data stays visible for the session. `tests/test_main.py::TestRootManagedSettingsStartup::test_missing_or_corrupt_settings_keep_commands_and_gate_model_work[*]` (ids unchanged): corrupt/invalid cache is absent, untouched at load, never quarantined, replaced by the first successful refresh. Other root nodes in `test_main.py`, `test_spawn_agent.py`, `test_execution_plan.py` and `test_root_composition_amended.py` read the cache path instead of `models-config.toml` for their unchanged "no persistence change" assertions.
- **F3 helper adaptation.** `RootHarness.start` seeds the cache with `ModelCache` (plus optional `legacy_settings` bytes placed at the retired path to prove it is untouched).
- **User-facing copy.** `telegram_formatter.py`: the unsaved-refresh line now reads "Refresh was not saved to the model cache — showing this session's results only."
- **Future ownership.** Startup selection, stale-connection filtering and bootstrap stay with 3.5; first-start discovery rules and page semantics with 3.6; supplement/provenance removal with 3.7; the `models-config.toml` existence notice with 10.3; `ModelSettings`/`preference=None` seams with later 5.x/10.x.
- **Body-hit scan.** 43 new nodes plus consumers of the changed harness gained or lost hits (see `executed_3_4` in the JSON). Audit: `live=5453 ledger=5453 counts={keep:4565,rewrite:496,retire:392} adds=47 problems=[]`. The audit tool is unchanged.

## Task 3.5 execution record

Task 3.5 implements startup selection for a preference-backed registry (ADR-0040): a saved, well-formed selector naming a configured connection is the exact active selection whether or not it is cached; a missing preference file makes the first full canonical ID in ascending ordinal order active in memory only (re-derived at every restart, never saved, never remembered); a saved selection that is invalid, wrong-typed, corrupt, unreadable or names an unconfigured connection leaves the selection unset with a bounded, content-free notice (`ModelRegistry.selection_notice`, `saved_selection_unusable`), no automatic choice, preserved bytes and no startup refresh (`main._refresh_model_catalog`). Cached candidates of unconfigured connections were already filtered at construction. `preference=None` registries keep the predecessor behaviour (no new legacy fallback).

- **Added keep nodes (30 + 7 in correction 1, classes 523/524, basis `added-3.5`)**: 18 in `tests/test_model_registry.py` (class 523) and 12 in `tests/test_main.py::TestRootStartupSelection` (class 524). They implement the four planned 3.5 add rows under different names (`adds[]` rows stay `planned-not-implemented` because the unchanged audit requires it). Root tests assert zero discovery fetches and zero provider HTTP calls for invalid/unreadable/unconfigured saved selections with a known and an empty cache; unreadable is a real OS failure (a directory at the preference path), not permission mocking.
- **Rewrite in place (dispositions unchanged)**. The pre-3.5 policy "a missing preference or an uncached saved id leaves nothing active" is retired where it was asserted: `test_preference_is_the_only_selection_source_and_sink` now proves `default_model` is not read through the ordinal-first choice; `test_missing_main_keeps_commands_available_without_default_or_model_work[absent|removed]`, `TestRootManagedSettingsStartup::test_missing_or_corrupt_settings_keep_commands_and_gate_model_work[*]` and every root test that used a missing preference or a configured uncached id as its "no active Main" state now use an unusable saved selection (`INVALID_SAVED`, `UNCONFIGURED_ID`) or, where the startup refresh is required, assert the in-memory first candidate. Refusal, no-I/O, no-leak and graph-independence assertions are unchanged. Details per node: `executed_3_5.rewritten_in_place` in the JSON. No test was deleted or skipped.
- **F3/F4-catalog helper adaptation.** `RootHarness.start` gains `preference_bytes`, `preference_unreadable`, `write_cache` and `removed_connection`; `INVALID_SAVED`, `UNCONFIGURED_ID` and `remembered_for()` are new. The registry unit helper is named `_build_world` (a name the unchanged tool already maps to F4-catalog).
- **Correction 1 (oracle P1).** The automatic choice is now the ordinal minimum of the *initially loaded*, connection-filtered cache, captured in `ModelRegistry.__init__` (`_initial_automatic_choice`); a startup refresh that replaces or empties the catalog no longer changes it (the active id may then be uncached; no record is synthesized, admission is unchanged). Only an empty initial cache derives the choice from refreshed candidates. 7 keep nodes added to classes 523/524 (4 registry, 3 in `TestRootInitialCacheChoice` incl. a real-`main._run` replacement and a valid-empty-inventory case); harness gains `extra_cached`. Audit: `live=5490 ledger=5490 counts={keep:4602,rewrite:496,retire:392} adds=47 problems=[]`; details in `executed_3_5.correction1`. README now states that an uncached active selection cannot run work yet.
- **Known follow-up gaps (not 3.5).** A saved configured selector that is not cached is *active* in `view()` but `admit`/`admit_scheduled` still fail closed with `no_active_selection` (the `active_id in self._models` gate), and a later `refresh()` that persists clears an active id missing from the inventory; both belong to 4.1 (selector admission) and 3.6 (refresh never changes selection). The unconditional startup refresh for a usable or missing preference is narrowed by 3.6.
- **Body-hit scan.** 30 new nodes plus consumers of the changed harness gained or lost hits (`executed_3_5` in the JSON). Audit before correction 1: `live=5483 ledger=5483 counts={keep:4595,rewrite:496,retire:392} adds=47 problems=[]`. The audit tool is unchanged.

## Task 3.6 execution record

Baseline: 5490 collected nodes (keep 4602, rewrite 496, retire 392). Current: **5505** collected nodes = 5490 − 4 renamed-away + 4 rename targets + **15 new** (keep 4617, rewrite 496, retire 392). The 15 new keep nodes are 14 in `tests/test_model_registry.py` (class 525, basis `added-3.6`) and 1 root node in `tests/test_main.py` (class 526: `TestRootStartupComposition::test_first_start_all_provider_failure_keeps_commands_and_refuses_work_locally`). No baseline class record (id, file, basis, disposition, destination, assertion) changed.

Renames (same baseline class, disposition and destination; only the node suffix changed):

| Old | New | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| `test_main.py::TestRootGraphStartupContainment::test_graph_resolves_stale_last_good_entry_after_failed_provider_refresh` | `…::test_graph_resolves_cached_stale_entry_without_any_startup_discovery` | a cached entry means no startup rediscovery (ADR-0040) |
| `test_main.py::TestRootInitialCacheChoice::test_cached_minimum_stays_active_when_discovery_replaces_the_catalog` | `…::test_cached_minimum_is_active_without_any_startup_discovery` | discovery cannot replace a cached catalog at startup |
| `test_main.py::TestRootInitialCacheChoice::test_valid_empty_inventory_keeps_the_cached_choice` | `…::test_valid_empty_refresh_after_first_start_keeps_the_choice_and_preference` | empty inventory now arrives by deliberate refresh |
| `test_model_registry.py::test_valid_empty_inventory_deactivates_but_remembers_selection_and_rediscovery_does_not_restore` | `…::test_valid_empty_inventory_clears_candidates_but_keeps_selection_and_rediscovery_does_not_change_it` | refresh never changes the selection |

Retired assertions (policy, not coverage): "a valid empty inventory deactivates the active selection" (twice), startup-refresh-after-saved-selection ordering/report assertions, and the saved-preference seeding assertions in the first-start root tests. Each surviving owner is the renamed or new node listed above. `body_scan` keys follow the renames, hits/tags for the 19 touched nodes were recomputed independently, and 31 consumers of the changed root helpers (18 + 13 in `tests/test_scheduler_failure_classification.py`) gained an `F3` dependency tag (tag only). Full maps are in the JSON key `executed_3_6`.

**Audit limitation.** The original external auditor and its temporary directories were purged and are unavailable, so the original audit was not run and its equivalence is not verified. The evidence is a separate read-only standard-library check (multiset of a fresh collection vs the ledger groups, class resolution, duplicates, dispositions, retire destinations, planned adds, AST comparison of changed tests, needle hits calibrated against existing entries: 145/179 exact on sampled nodes, mismatches are module-level constants the check does not model). It is not a reproduction of the original scanner.

**Round 4 (test-only, after the failed parent gate).** Three plan-step root tests (`test_execution_plan.py::TestProductionRootPlanAdmission::test_plan_step_snapshots_active_main_despite_opaque_model_arg`, `test_root_composition_amended.py::test_plan_step_inherits_main_through_real_plan_executor`, `…::test_failed_plan_step_triggers_real_diagnostic_on_inherited_main`) failed with `output_cap_unavailable`: the cached record has no provider-reported `max_tokens` and startup no longer rediscovers a cached catalog. Each now runs a deliberate `registry.refresh()` in a local hold before its spies; no fake budget or global fixture change. `test_sub_agent_supervisor.py::TestTurnOwnershipObligations::test_timeout_cancellation_accounts_while_cleanup_is_pending` (clean at HEAD, its baseline) is a baseline race fix, not a 3.6 feature: a local gated runner stays inside `run()` after observing cancellation, so the "cleanup pending" state is a real precondition; two wait assertions were added, none removed. Node count stays 5505; classes, dispositions and assertions are unchanged; 6 rewritten `test_main.py` root nodes gained the `F3` tag. Manual closure replaces the unavailable original auditor (AST assertion enumeration and a needle-superset check over 13 changed existing nodes).

| `tests/execution_harness.py` | ScriptedLLM/run_react harness | keep; budget hooks removed in 10.1 |

Fixture rule F1 is broader than `minimal_config`: `make_builtin_executor`, `run_model_fixtures.py`, module-local `AGENT_CFG`, `_World`, `_make_controller`, direct test bodies and parameter decorators may carry obsolete keys. The per-node body hit/tag map enumerates consumers; update each direct-body assertion or helper on the owning slice, and rerun all tagged consumers. No test node retires merely due to fixture changes.

## Implementation follow-ups within frozen scope (not user-choice blockers)

- **O1:** Keep `test_telegram_models.py::TestLegacyPathPreserved` conservatively until the actual 9.x surface implementation is reviewed; if the no-registry compatibility path proves obsolete, retire *only then* with an explicit ledger update. No new user choice is required now.
- **O2:** `migrate_models.py` symlink refusal at ~808–818 is migration-only hardening. Current `model_settings.py` has no corresponding check and the frozen design does not require adding one. Retire those migration-only tests; do **not** claim ADR-0028/private-home tests cover a new preference symlink check.
- **O3:** Existing summary **prompt-input** head/tail rendering is distinct from prohibited truncation of published goal/recent/summary. Keep `TestMiddleFormatting`; confirm at task 6.4 by exercising actual published history (not as a user decision now).
- **O4:** `tests/test_context_manager.py` is a reasonable new owner file for `context_manager.py` tests in task 2.3. The parent may choose the owner-suite name at relocation; this is not a product-scope decision.
- **O5:** The body-level inventory is complete for the static needles above, including shared and module-local helpers. Future implementation may expose a further assertion; update the ledger **before** removal. Do not treat the audit as permission to delete a whole suite.

## Task 3.7 execution record (unreviewed; parent owns final gate)

Pre-edit baseline: **5505** collected, 5503 passed, two baseline skips, two warnings; focused owner baseline 500 passed. Focused new image/native/no-public tests were RED first (ten failures: four local vision refusals, five old discovery-constructor requirements, one root supplement request). Code changes and rewritten suites are now focused GREEN: 1270 passed; exact Ruff and Vulture clean. No complete project pytest run or final gate claimed here.

Task-owned production: `model_discovery.py` contacts only configured provider endpoints; its public HTTP client, supplement merge and `ProviderInventory.supplemental` are gone. `main.py` owns one discovery client instead of two. Registry/document remove `SupplementRecord` and provenance fields and keep provider-reported display fields; `ModelCache` writes only candidate IDs/provider freshness/display fields. The retired TOML `ModelSettings` reader remains for converter/backfill until 10.x: it may decode legacy `sources`/`supplemental` as obsolete input, **discards** them and never writes them or passes them to runtime/cache. Real HTTP boundary and cache tests prove no new supplement network call or persistence. `llm_client.py` no longer checks pinned metadata vision in chat or preview; native admission retains genuine adapter unsupported-native rejection. No budget, compaction, selector membership, scheduler or graph policy is changed. `prompt_builder.py`, `telegram_formatter.py` and `README.md` remove provenance/source labeling. Provider-reported **vision/tool-calling flags** are display-only; discovered token limits still affect current request budgeting, required output caps and local fit rejection pending 6.x.

Fresh node accounting (JSON `executed_3_7` is authoritative): **5505 − 25 old IDs + 29 new IDs = 5509** (13 rename pairs, 12 retired task-owned policy IDs, 16 genuinely new IDs across seven keep classes). Five generic `test_model_settings` scalar/type and round-trip nodes in historical retire class 135 were reclassified in existing rewrite class 136 before retirement; their assertions continue to run. Original baseline class definitions remain byte-equivalent; only the affected group membership and new keep classes change. The old provider inventory refresh node and prompt provenance node were retired **only as supplement/provenance owners** and new keep nodes retain their generic replacement/renderer assertions; the invalid old supplement TOML fixture was replaced with a strict wrong-type rejection and the old legacy row has a separate explicit discard/never-rewrite test. The second discovery-client-construction failure branch was retired because that client no longer exists; interrupt-during-provider-refresh, root-owned resource close, and graph shutdown ownership branches still run. The scheduled vision-unverified local rejection parameter was retired; context/provider-failure and scheduled pin/no-fallback branches survive. Image tests were inverted to assert one pinned provider call with image wire on false/unknown metadata; real provider rejection remains sanitized, no retarget. Unsupported-native adapter before-I/O stays tested independently.

Changed helper closure: `ModelMetadata`, `CatalogDocument`, `ProviderInventory` and `ModelDiscovery` positional/keyword consumers were enumerated via AST and adapted without swapping the production registry/resolver with application mocks. `tests/root_startup_harness.py` now treats `models.dev` as an unexpected HTTP destination; all affected root composition and lifecycle branches execute. **Every one of the 29 renamed/new live IDs** was reconciled individually in JSON `executed_3_7.manual_known_contacts.nodes`: 28 have explicit retained known hits in `body_scan.hits`, and the direct metadata-record test has none. Known tags total **F1=12, F3=2, F4-catalog=11** (a node may carry two tags). F1 covers `tests/run_model_fixtures.py:make_run_model_client` and `test_run_model_client.py:_client` retaining `AgentConfig(ctx_max_tokens)`; F3 covers `tests/root_startup_harness.py:RootHarness`; F4-catalog covers registry, Telegram, prompt and execution-harness catalog builders. Old-name keys were removed; only contacts actually removed with the public supplement/provenance implementation were dropped. Historical `body_hits_deferred.old_records_removed_or_renamed` remains as an old-ID snapshot, **not** a live-node substitute. Explicit known contacts are a manual bounded reconciliation, not a rerun of the unavailable historical scanner or proof of complete transitive dependency closure.

## Task 6.7 prerequisite correction (cancellation precedence before failure logging; candidate)

Approved by the user after the oracle held 6.7 on a real defect: the client emitted ERROR `LLM_FAILED` before `_summarize_on_pin` could convert a loop-only cancel into `LLMCancelledError` (ADR-0043). This **supersedes** the pass-1 wording "pre-existing order-dependent flake" above: it was a production defect from 6.6, invisible to the earlier tests (unbootstrapped logging) and to the first gate. Fix: context-local cancel binding in `llm_client` plus `_summarize_on_pin` binding `ctx.cancel_event`; cancellation wins before failure classification; ordinary uncancelled failures keep the ERROR `LLM_FAILED` event. RED on unfixed code under the real `setup_bootstrap()` pipeline: 7 failed/5 passed; GREEN 12 passed.

36 new keep nodes (class 561, `tests/test_cancellation_logging_6_7_prereq.py`); 6063 -> 6099; classes 0..560 unchanged; counts keep 5278, rewrite 484, retire 337. Changed existing nodes (IDs unchanged): three class-559 nodes in `tests/test_compaction_failure_6_6.py` replaced the unbootstrapped-only `no ERROR` assertion with `_assert_only_client_request_failures` (before/after lists: JSON `body_scan.changed_owner_audit_6_7_prereq`). Mutants: 7 of 7 killed (restore old order, omit loop binding, client-only, ctx-only, never-reset, genuine failure downgraded or turned into cancel). Pass 3 added 24 nodes to class 561 (36 total), now 6099 collected, and four copied-tree test-only mutants all killed. The bootstrap fixture detaches saved handlers before bootstrap can close them and closes only its own handlers on exit; `LLM_FAILED` proof requires structured event_type plus llm_client identity (not substring), spoof controls and handler-preservation nodes added. Contacts: `body_scan.new_6_7_prereq` (local `production_logging` fixture; F1 `make_run_model_client` manual). Limits as before; inherited stale `TestProviderCredentialInheritance` entry untouched.

## Task 9.3 execution record (candidate; awaiting independent review and parent gate; no checkbox)

6338 -> 6360 collected: +22 keep nodes in new class 571 (`tests/test_models_selection_tokens_9_3.py`), 0 retired, 1 renamed in place (`test_select_callback_reports_reopen_when_target_removed_before_redemption` -> `test_select_callback_selects_target_removed_before_redemption`, class stays rewrite); classes 0..570 unchanged. keep 5578 -> 5600, rewrite 457, retire 303.

Real gap found and fixed: `ModelRegistry.select` required cache membership, so a valid token for a candidate removed by a later refresh was refused (spec: the cache is not a whitelist). It now validates only the first-slash shape and configured connection (`parse_selector`); the callback maps malformed/unconfigured selectors to the existing reopen text. The token store (`telegram_model_selection.py`) already met the 64-byte, scope, TTL and not-consumed contracts and is unchanged; the new tests characterize those on the split cache+preference wiring. README's "rechecked against the current inventory" sentence was corrected.

Rewritten old-policy assertions (JSON `executed_9_3.rewritten_assertions`): the removed-before-redemption callback node, `test_missing_settings_do_not_infer_selection_and_explicit_admission_needs_no_main`, `test_ordinary_selection_and_admission_use_exact_canonical_ids_only` x4 (select code now equals admit code) and `test_failed_first_start_leaves_empty_state_that_a_deliberate_refresh_and_select_recover`. Exact-id, no-normalization and persistence assertions are unchanged.

RED: 3 of 22 failed before the edit: 2 unambiguous real-gap failures plus 1 confounded by a test defect (not re-recorded after correction); 19 passed (1 vacuously, later strengthened). Oracle follow-up: the long-identity test no longer bans the short substring org; it mints org-prefixed tokens deterministically and asserts full identity plus structure (22 nodes, collection unchanged at 6360). 10 of 10 copied-tree mutants killed under Python 3.14.6 (one mutant was replaced because it was detail-free; disclosed in JSON).
